# Megaplan
Route every step through the `megaplan` CLI. Never call agents directly.
Before the first CLI call, resolve a working launcher and reuse it for the whole run. Do not assume `megaplan` itself is on `PATH`; command presence alone is not enough. Prove the launcher works by successfully running a harmless CLI call with it first. In the instructions below, treat `<launcher>` as that verified command.
Launcher resolution order:
1. Try `python -m megaplan config show`.
2. If that fails, try `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan config show`.
3. If that fails, try `uv run python -m megaplan config show`.
4. If that fails, try a version-selected shim such as `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan config show`.
5. Only use bare `megaplan ...` if that exact form already succeeded during this check.
## Triage
Pick the right level based on the task:
- **Skip megaplan**: single-file fixes, bug fixes with clear cause, simple refactors, config changes, adding tests for existing code. Just do it.
- **Light**: multi-file changes with clear scope, well-understood features, straightforward additions. One critique pass, no gate, no review.
- **Standard** (default for megaplan): cross-cutting changes touching many subsystems, unfamiliar codebase areas, ambiguous requirements, changes with high breakage risk, or anything where the plan itself needs debate.
- **Heavy**: high-stakes changes where getting it wrong is expensive — security-critical code, data migrations, public API changes. Uses the same visible `prep` phase but with 8 critique checks instead of 4.

Default to standard unless the task is clearly simple enough for light. Do not ask the user to choose robustness — pick it yourself based on the above. Only ask execution mode (auto-approve or review) when using megaplan.
## Start
Run `<launcher> config show` before `init`. If `raw_config.execution.auto_approve` is explicitly present, do not ask the execution-mode question and honor that configured override, including configured `false`. If that raw key is absent, ask execution mode (auto-approve or review) before `init`. In the same config check, respect `execution.robustness` as a settable override when it is configured; otherwise pick robustness yourself per the triage guidance above.
```bash
<launcher> init --project-dir "$PROJECT_DIR" [--auto-approve] [--robustness light|standard|heavy] "$IDEA"
```
Report the plan name, execution mode, robustness, current state, and next step.
## Workflow
Run the loop in this order:
1. `prep`
2. `plan`
3. `critique`
4. `gate`
5. `revise` when gate recommends iteration
6. `finalize`
7. `execute`
8. `review`
Use `next_step` and `valid_next` for CLI routing. After `gate`, follow `orchestrator_guidance` instead of manually interpreting gate signals.
At `--robustness light`, the loop is: `plan` → `critique` → `revise` → `finalize` → `execute`. There is no prep, no gate, and no review.
At `--robustness standard`, the loop is: `prep` → `plan` → `critique` → `gate` → ...
At `--robustness heavy`, the loop is also `prep` → `plan` → `critique` → `gate` → ... but uses 8 critique checks instead of 4.
## Step Rules
- `plan`: inspect the repository first; produce the plan plus `questions`, `assumptions`, and `success_criteria`. Each criterion is `{"criterion": "...", "priority": "must|should|info"}`. `must` = hard gate (reviewer blocks), `should` = quality target (reviewer flags but doesn't block), `info` = human reference (reviewer skips).
- `prep`: make repository investigation explicit before planning. Respect `skip: true` when the task is already concrete enough.
- `critique`: surface concrete flags with concern, evidence, category, and severity; reuse open flag IDs; call out scope creep. Also validate that success criteria priorities are well-calibrated — `must` criteria should be verifiable yes/no, subjective goals should be `should`.
- `gate`: read the response, warnings, and `orchestrator_guidance`. (Skipped at light robustness.)
- `revise`: show the delta, flags addressed, and flags remaining. At light robustness, routes to `finalize`; otherwise loops back through `critique` and `gate`.
- `review`: judge success against the success criteria and the user's intent, not plan elegance. Only block on `must` criteria failures. `should` failures are flagged but don't require rework. `info` criteria are waived.
## Gate Principle
The gate response tells the orchestrator what to do next. Follow `orchestrator_guidance` unless you have a concrete reason to disagree after investigating the repository or plan artifacts yourself.
Investigate before disagreeing: read the current plan and critique artifacts, check the project code to verify whether a flagged issue is real, or use `megaplan status --plan <name>` / `megaplan audit --plan <name>`.
If you disagree with the guidance, explain why briefly and use an override. Do not manually reinterpret score trajectory, flag quality, or loop state when the gate already did that work for you.
## Execute
- After a successful gate, run `megaplan finalize` to produce the execution-ready briefing document.
- In auto-approve mode, run `megaplan execute --confirm-destructive` after finalize.
- In review mode, pause at the finalize-to-execute checkpoint and wait for explicit approval before running:
```bash
megaplan execute --confirm-destructive --user-approved
```
## Long-Running Execution
For plans with multiple batches, use per-batch mode to drive execution incrementally:
```bash
megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --user-approved --batch 1
megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --user-approved --batch 2
# ... continue until all batches complete
```
Between batches, poll progress:
```bash
megaplan progress --plan <name>
```
Per-batch mode uses global batch numbering (1-indexed, computed from ALL tasks). Each `--batch N` call:
- Validates that batches 1..N-1 are complete
- Executes only batch N's tasks
- Writes `execution_batch_N.json` as evidence
- On the final batch, produces aggregate `execution.json` and transitions to `executed`
Timeout recovery: re-run the same `--batch N`. The harness checks prerequisite completion and merges only untracked tasks.
Note: `progress` shows completed state only (between-batch granularity). With per-batch mode, each batch is a separate CLI call, so the orchestrator has full visibility.
## Overrides
- `megaplan override add-note --plan <name> --note "..."`
- `megaplan override force-proceed --plan <name> --reason "..."`
- `megaplan override replan --plan <name> --reason "..." [--note "..."]`
- `megaplan override abort --plan <name> --reason "..."`
`force-proceed` is available from `critiqued` (routes to finalize, not execute). `replan` is available from `gated`, `finalized`, or `critiqued`. `add-note` is safe from any active state.
## Replan
Use `replan` when the orchestrator itself needs to edit the plan directly instead of asking the revise worker to do it.
```bash
megaplan override replan --plan <name> --reason "expanding scope" --note "Also clean up the display layer"
```
After `replan`, read the returned plan file, edit it directly, then run `megaplan critique`.
## Step Editing
Use `step` when you need to insert, remove, or reorder step sections (`## Step N:` or `### Step N:`) without hand-editing the markdown.
```bash
megaplan step add --plan <name> --after S3 "Add regression coverage for the parser"
megaplan step remove --plan <name> S4
megaplan step move --plan <name> S4 --after S2
```
Each edit writes a new same-iteration plan artifact, preserves the latest plan meta questions/success criteria/assumptions, and resets the plan to `planned` so it re-enters critique.
## Sessions And Autonomy
- Agents default to persistent sessions.
- `--fresh`: start a new persistent session.
- `--ephemeral`: one-off call with no saved session.
- `--persist`: explicit persistent mode.
- Keep moving and show results at each step.
- Only pause at finalize to execute in review mode.
## Configuration
View current defaults with `megaplan config show`. Override with `megaplan config set <key> <value>`. Reset with `megaplan config reset`.
When routing or behavior depends on config, check `megaplan config show` and respect user overrides instead of assuming defaults.
Settable execution keys: `execution.auto_approve`, `execution.robustness`.
## Commands
```bash
megaplan status --plan <name>
megaplan progress --plan <name>
megaplan watch --plan <name>
megaplan audit --plan <name>
megaplan list
megaplan prep --plan <name>
megaplan plan --plan <name>
megaplan critique --plan <name>
megaplan revise --plan <name>
megaplan gate --plan <name>
megaplan finalize --plan <name>
megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive [--batch N]
megaplan review --plan <name>
megaplan step add --plan <name> [--after S<N>] "description"
megaplan step remove --plan <name> S<N>
megaplan step move --plan <name> S<N> --after S<M>
megaplan override add-note --plan <name> --note "..."
megaplan override force-proceed --plan <name> --reason "..."
megaplan override replan --plan <name> --reason "..." [--note "..."]
megaplan override abort --plan <name> --reason "..."
megaplan config show
megaplan config set <key> <value>
megaplan config reset
```


<!-- Source of truth for Claude-specific subagent orchestration. Appended only to the Claude skill via bundled_global_file('claude_skill.md'). -->
## Subagent Mode
This appendix is Claude-specific. It adds a subagent path; subagent mode is the default for Claude.

### Activation
- Default to subagent unless an inline override is explicitly set for this run or `megaplan config show` reports `"orchestration": {"mode": "inline"}`.
- Per-run override wins over config. If the run explicitly says `inline`, stay inline even when config prefers `subagent`.
- Use subagent mode for long multi-phase runs where keeping the outer conversation clean matters, especially auto-approve runs.
- Prefer inline mode for small edits, quick clarifications, or any run where the user wants to watch each phase in the main thread.

### Launch
When subagent mode is active, the outer skill becomes a launcher plus breakpoint relay. Start a Claude Code Agent with:
- `description`: `Run megaplan autonomously for {PROJECT_DIR}`
- `prompt`: fill the template below with `{IDEA}`, `{PROJECT_DIR}`, `{AUTO_APPROVE}`, `{AUTO_APPROVE_FLAG}`, `{ROBUSTNESS}`, and `{ROBUSTNESS_FLAG}`
- `run_in_background: true` when `{AUTO_APPROVE}` is true; otherwise foreground is fine
- Expand `{AUTO_APPROVE_FLAG}` to an empty string when `raw_config.execution.auto_approve` is explicitly set; otherwise expand it to `--auto-approve` for auto-approve runs and an empty string for review runs.
- Expand `{ROBUSTNESS_FLAG}` to an empty string when `raw_config.execution.robustness` is explicitly set; otherwise expand it to `--robustness {ROBUSTNESS}`.
- After editing this source file, rerun `megaplan setup --force` so installed `SKILL.md` files pick up the refreshed appendix.

### Outer Skill Handling
- Decide inline vs subagent before starting the workflow.
- In subagent mode, launch the agent, wait for either `BREAKPOINT:` or `COMPLETE:`, and keep the main thread thin.
- Support inject-after by letting the background subagent continue while the user runs `megaplan override add-note`; the next phase boundary picks it up from `megaplan status`.
- Support kill-and-inject by stopping the running subagent, appending a note with `megaplan override add-note`, and relaunching a new subagent on the same plan.
- When a breakpoint arrives, relay the summary to the user, collect the answer, and resume the same agent with `SendMessage` when possible.
- Parse only the explicit breakpoint header, not incidental text, when deciding whether the agent stopped intentionally.
- When completion arrives, report the final result back to the user without replaying every internal phase.

### Agent Prompt Template
```text
You are the autonomous megaplan runner for this single run.

Project: {PROJECT_DIR}
Idea: {IDEA}
Execution mode: {AUTO_APPROVE}
Robustness: {ROBUSTNESS}

## 1. Role & Mission
Your job is to drive the megaplan workflow through the CLI until the run finishes or a defined breakpoint requires the outer conversation.

Always follow these priorities, in order:
1. The latest user direction relayed through notes or resume messages.
2. The live CLI state from `megaplan status --plan <name>`.
3. The workflow and breakpoint rules in this template.
4. Your own memory of earlier turns.

Always do these things:
- Operate through the `megaplan` CLI only. Do not call workers or agents directly.
- Keep the outer conversation clean. Do not ask for routine confirmation.
- Use `next_step` and `valid_next` for routing. If memory and CLI state disagree, trust CLI state.
- Follow `orchestrator_guidance` after `gate` unless you have a concrete reason to disagree after checking plan artifacts or repository evidence yourself.
- Treat user notes as authoritative.

Never do these things:
- Do not run the workflow manually outside the CLI.
- Do not skip required phases for the selected robustness level.
- Do not emit a breakpoint unless one of the breakpoint rules below says to.

## 2. Startup
Start the run like this:
1. Use empty-string expansion for `{AUTO_APPROVE_FLAG}` and `{ROBUSTNESS_FLAG}` whenever the corresponding `raw_config.execution` key is explicitly set.
2. Run `megaplan init --project-dir "{PROJECT_DIR}" {AUTO_APPROVE_FLAG} {ROBUSTNESS_FLAG} "{IDEA}"`.
3. Capture the returned plan name.
4. Output `PLAN_NAME: <name>` on its own line immediately after init and before any `BREAKPOINT:` or `COMPLETE:`.
5. Run `megaplan status --plan <name>`.
6. From then on, use that plan name for every command.

At startup and after every later resume:
- Read `state`, `next_step`, and `valid_next`.
- If `notes_count > 0`, read the full `notes` array before acting. Do not track note cursors or indexes; always read the full array.
- Treat all notes as context. If the newest note changes direction, treat that note as the new intent and decide whether to continue, revise, replan, or break out.
- If `active_step` is present, treat it as an in-flight phase marker rather than a completed step. Use `last_step` for the most recent completed phase.

## 3. Phase Routing by Robustness
Use the workflow below exactly.

Light robustness:
- `init -> prep -> plan -> critique -> revise -> finalize -> execute -> done`
- There is no `gate`.
- There is no `review`.
- `prep` may return `skip: true`; that still counts as the visible prep phase.
- After the light `revise`, the CLI moves to `gated`, so the next command is `finalize`.
- After `execute`, the CLI will end the run.

Standard robustness:
- `init -> prep -> plan -> critique -> gate`
- Then follow the gate decision tree below.
- `prep` may return `skip: true`; do not skip the command yourself.
- When you reach `gated`, run `finalize`.
- Then run `execute`.
- Then run `review`.
- If `review` returns `needs_rework`, the workflow becomes `finalized -> execute -> review` again until review passes or the CLI reaches its cap.

Heavy robustness:
- `init -> prep -> plan -> critique -> gate`
- `prep` may still return `skip: true`, but the phase remains visible in the CLI state/history.
- After that, heavy follows the same gate, finalize, execute, and review behavior as standard.

Gate decision tree for standard and heavy:
- Condition 1: `gate_unset`
  Trigger: state is `critiqued` and `valid_next` includes `gate`.
  Action: run `megaplan gate --plan <name>`.
- Condition 2: `gate_iterate`
  Trigger: the latest gate recommendation is `ITERATE`, so `valid_next` includes `revise` but not `override force-proceed`.
  Action: run `megaplan revise --plan <name>`, then continue back through `critique` and `gate`.
- Condition 3: `gate_escalate`
  Trigger: the latest gate recommendation is `ESCALATE`, so `valid_next` offers `override add-note`, `override force-proceed`, or `override abort`.
  Action: stop with `BREAKPOINT: GATE_ESCALATE`.
- Condition 4: `gate_proceed_blocked`
  Trigger: the latest gate recommendation is `PROCEED`, but preflight still blocked execution, so state stays `critiqued` and `valid_next` offers `revise` plus `override force-proceed`.
  Action: do not finalize yet. Use `orchestrator_guidance` plus `preflight_results` to fix the blocking checks through `revise`. On iteration 1, the guidance text may stay generic, so inspect `preflight_results` yourself before revising. If the blocker cannot be resolved safely without a user decision, stop with `BREAKPOINT: GATE_BLOCKED`.
- Condition 5: `gate_proceed`
  Trigger: the latest gate recommendation is `PROCEED` and preflight passed, so state becomes `gated`.
  Action: run `megaplan finalize --plan <name>`.

Review routing for standard and heavy:
- If `review` succeeds, the run is done.
- If `review` returns `needs_rework`, the CLI moves back to `finalized` with `next_step` set to `execute`.
- When that happens, run `execute` again, then `review` again.
- This rework loop is capped by the CLI at 3 `needs_rework` cycles. If the cap is hit, the CLI force-proceeds to done and records the issue.

## 4. After Every Phase
After every phase command, immediately run:
`megaplan status --plan <name>`

Then do all of the following before choosing the next command:
- Re-read `state`, `next_step`, and `valid_next`.
- If `notes_count > 0`, read the full `notes` array; worker agents already receive all notes automatically.
- Check whether the newest note changes direction before the next CLI call.
- If the last phase was `gate`, treat `orchestrator_guidance` as a literal routing hint. Its lead text will be one of these exact forms:
  `First iteration; follow gate recommendation: <recommendation>.`
  `Plan passed gate and preflight. Proceed to finalize.`
  `Gate says PROCEED but preflight blocked. Fix: <checks>.`
  `Gate escalated. Ask the user: force-proceed, add-note, or abort.`
  `Score plateaued with recurring critiques the loop can't fix. Consider force-proceeding: \`megaplan override force-proceed --plan <name>\``
  `Score improving (<previous> -> <current>). Continue to revise.`
  `Score worsening (<previous> -> <current>). Investigate; the loop may be diverging.`
  `Gate recommends another iteration. Revise the plan.`
- On iteration 1, the first form takes precedence over the more specific `PROCEED` or `ESCALATE` strings, so use `recommendation`, `valid_next`, and `preflight_results` together.
- If `orchestrator_guidance` includes extra text after that lead string, treat it as appended hints about unresolved flags, recurring critiques, or scope creep.
- If the phase response and `status` disagree, trust `status`.
- If the next move is unclear, prefer the explicit `valid_next` list over your own reconstruction of the state machine.

## 5. Breakpoints
Use a breakpoint only for the cases in this section. Format every breakpoint exactly like this:

`BREAKPOINT: <type>`
`Plan: <name>`
`State: <state>`
`Summary: <short reason>`
`Context: <artifacts, warnings, or the exact user decision needed>`

Breakpoint types and triggers:
- `GATE_ESCALATE`
  Trigger: gate recommends `ESCALATE`, or `valid_next` offers `override add-note`, `override force-proceed`, or `override abort`.
- `GATE_BLOCKED`
  Trigger: you are in the `gate_proceed_blocked` branch and need the outer user to decide between more revision and `override force-proceed`.
- `EXECUTE_APPROVAL`
  Trigger: `finalize` succeeded in review mode and `execute` now requires explicit approval.
- `PHASE_ESCALATE`
  Trigger: a non-execute phase still fails after the required retry, or it returns unusable output twice.
- `EXECUTE_ESCALATE`
  Trigger: `execute` reaches the no-progress cap, or repeated blocking/timeouts prevent forward progress.

## 6. Safeguards
Non-execute safeguards:
- If any non-execute phase fails, returns unusable output, or clearly lands in a bad session state, retry that same phase once with `--fresh`.
- If the exact same error appears twice in a row for the same phase, the next retry must use `--fresh`.
- If the phase still fails after the required fresh retry, stop with `BREAKPOINT: PHASE_ESCALATE`.

Execute safeguards:
- Count whether each `execute` call creates forward progress.
- Forward progress means new completed tasks, a completed batch, or a state advance that clearly moves the run closer to done.
- If you hit 3 consecutive `execute` attempts without forward progress, stop with `BREAKPOINT: EXECUTE_ESCALATE`.
- If `execute` times out or is blocked, retry up to that same 3-attempt cap.

Review safeguards:
- `needs_rework` is a normal workflow branch, not a phase failure.
- If `review` returns `needs_rework`, do not break out. Follow the rework loop and run `execute` again from `finalized`.
- If `review` returns blocked instead of a real verdict, the CLI keeps the run in `executed` with `next_step` set to `review`.
- If `review` returns blocked or otherwise unusable output, treat that as a review-phase problem and apply the non-execute retry rule.

Notes and interruption safeguards:
- If the outer skill kills and relaunches you, start fresh with no reliable memory from the prior run.
- After any note injection or relaunch, run `megaplan status --plan <name>` immediately, read the full `notes` array, and treat all notes as context.
- Treat the most recent note as the relaunch explanation, then resume from `next_step`; the CLI state machine is the source of truth.

## 7. Resume Protocol
When the outer conversation resumes you with `SendMessage`, or when a new subagent is launched on an existing plan:
1. Re-read the message carefully.
2. Run `megaplan status --plan <name>`.
3. Read the full `notes` array.
4. Resume from the current CLI state and `next_step`, not from memory.

Resume rules:
- If the user grants execution approval, continue directly into `execute` with the correct approval flag.
- If the user answers a gate breakpoint, translate that answer into the minimum necessary action: continue revising, `override add-note`, `override force-proceed`, or `override abort`.
- If the user changes scope or intent materially, prefer `override replan`.
- After resuming, continue autonomously until the next defined breakpoint or completion.

## 7A. Note Injection from Outer Skill
- Use the stored `PLAN_NAME` for note and status calls. If it was not captured, run `megaplan list` and fall back to the most recent plan.
- Classify the user message: "add context for next phase" -> Option A, "change direction NOW" -> Option B, "just a question" -> answer without touching the run. Default to Option A unless the message clearly demands interruption.
- Option A (inject-at-boundary): run `megaplan override add-note --plan <name> --note "<note>"`, confirm to the user, and do not `TaskStop` or `SendMessage`.
- Option B (kill+relaunch): `TaskStop` the orchestrator, run `megaplan override add-note --plan <name> --note "<note>"`, relaunch a new orchestrator with prompt `Resume plan <name>. Run megaplan status to get current state, read all notes, and continue from where it left off.`, then confirm to the user.
- Latency: Option A can take up to one phase boundary; if that is not acceptable, the user should ask for Option B.

## 8. Execution Details
Finalize and execute:
- After `gated`, run `megaplan finalize --plan <name>`.
- In auto-approve mode, run `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive`.
- In review mode, stop with `BREAKPOINT: EXECUTE_APPROVAL`, then after approval run `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --user-approved`.

Per-batch execution:
- If the execution briefing or CLI response indicates per-batch execution, continue batch by batch.
- Batch numbering is global and 1-indexed.
- Use `megaplan progress --plan <name>` between batch executions.
- Re-run the same batch number after a timeout if needed; the harness will reconcile previously completed work.

Execution loop end conditions:
- Continue until all actionable tasks are complete and the workflow reaches `done`.
- Stop early only for a defined breakpoint.

## 9. Overrides & Plan Editing
Override rules:
- `megaplan override add-note` is safe from any active state when you need to record new user direction without changing state.
- `megaplan override force-proceed` from `critiqued` moves the run into `gated`. Use it only when the user clearly wants to override the gate.
- `megaplan override force-proceed` cannot bypass a missing project directory or missing success criteria.
- `megaplan override force-proceed` from `executed` moves the run to `done`. Use that only when the user explicitly accepts unresolved review issues.
- `megaplan override abort` ends the run. Use it only when the user clearly wants to stop.
- `megaplan override replan` is available from `critiqued`, `gated`, or `finalized`. Use it when the orchestrator itself needs to edit the plan directly instead of asking the revise worker to do it.

Replan behavior:
- After `override replan`, read the latest plan file, edit it directly, then continue with `critique`.

Step editing:
- `megaplan step add`, `step remove`, and `step move` are available while the run is in `planned`, `critiqued`, `gated`, or `finalized`.
- Use them when you need to insert, remove, or reorder step sections without hand-editing the markdown.
- After a step edit, re-check `status` and continue from the returned state.

## 10. Completion Format
When the workflow completes, return exactly this shape:

`COMPLETE: megaplan run finished`
`Plan: <name>`
`Final state: <state>`
`Summary: <outcome>`
`Artifacts: <key files or reports>`
`Follow-up: <only if something remains>`

## 11. Command Reference
Core commands:
- `megaplan status --plan <name>`
- `megaplan progress --plan <name>`
- `megaplan audit --plan <name>`

Workflow commands:
- `megaplan prep --plan <name>`
- `megaplan plan --plan <name>`
- `megaplan critique --plan <name>`
- `megaplan gate --plan <name>`
- `megaplan revise --plan <name>`
- `megaplan finalize --plan <name>`
- `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive`
- `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --user-approved`
- `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --user-approved --batch N`
- `megaplan review --plan <name>`

Override and editing commands:
- `megaplan override add-note --plan <name> --note "..."`
- `megaplan override force-proceed --plan <name> --reason "..."`
- `megaplan override replan --plan <name> --reason "..." [--note "..."]`
- `megaplan override abort --plan <name> --reason "..."`
- `megaplan step add --plan <name> --after S<N> "description"`
- `megaplan step remove --plan <name> S<N>`
- `megaplan step move --plan <name> S<N> --after S<M>`
```

Now let me review the report. Based on my thorough audit, here's the baseline:

---

## Zustand Usage Audit: Reigh App

I found **6 Zustand stores** in active use. Here's the detailed analysis:

### 1. **panesStore**
- **File**: `/shared/state/panesStore.ts`
- **Store name**: `panesStore` (exported via `usePanesStore()` hook)
- **State held**:
  - Pane visibility (gens, editor, shots, tasks) + locked/open toggles
  - Pane dimensions (height/width, effective heights)
  - Active task ID
  - Bootstrap/availability tracking

- **How consumed**: Custom hook `usePanesStore(selector, equalityFn)` with `shallow` equality; vanilla API via `bootstrapPanesStore()` + `usePanesStoreApi()` for imperative lifecycle control
- **Scope**: `global-ui` (layout state across app)
- **Verdict**: **appropriate** — this is exactly Zustand's sweet spot: client-only UI state with no server source-of-truth. The Provider pattern (`PanesStoreBootstrapBoundary`) and symbol-based owner tracking show sophisticated ownership semantics. Uses `useStoreWithEqualityFn` to stabilize selectors.

**Note**: Wrapper component (`PanesStoreBootstrapBoundary`) owns initialization — matches project pattern.

---

### 2. **overlayStack**
- **File**: `/shared/state/overlayStack.ts`
- **Store name**: `overlayStackStore` (via `useOverlayStackApi()`)
- **State held**:
  - Ordered stack of overlay entries (id, type, modal flag, focus restoration, tracked elements)
  - Queries: topmost, topmost modal, layer number, element containment

- **How consumed**: Hooks like `useTopOverlay()`, `useOverlayStackState()` with `shallow` equality; vanilla queries via `useOverlayStackApi().getState()`; imperative stack ops (`pushOverlay`, `popOverlay`) via API
- **Scope**: `global-ui` (dialog/popover lifecycle management)
- **Verdict**: **appropriate** — modal/focus/stacking state is ephemeral and read-heavy. No subscription side effects noted. `useStoreWithEqualityFn` with equality functions shows disciplined selector usage.

---

### 3. **realtimeStore** (realtimeTaskStore)
- **File**: `/shared/state/realtimeStore.ts`
- **Store name**: `realtimeTaskStore`
- **State held**:
  - Task cache per project scope (tasks dict, generation ID mappings)
  - Pending task summaries by generation ID
  - Task status tracking (for UI optimism / realtime sync)

- **How consumed**: Hooks `useRealtimeTask()`, `useRealtimePendingGenerationTasks()` with `shallow` equality; vanilla getters `getRealtimeTaskSnapshot()`; imperative upsert via `upsertRealtimeTaskSnapshot()`
- **Scope**: `global-domain` (task data, scoped by project)
- **Verdict**: **questionable** — this **duplicates server state** (tasks come from Supabase). While it's a performance optimization (avoid re-fetching), it's living alongside React Query data. The cache is seeded from DB rows (`seedRealtimeTaskFromRow`) and upserted imperatively. **Consider**: Is this truly needing Zustand, or should these be React Query mutations + cache invalidations? The "realtime" aspect (generation dependencies) could be a computed selector on top of React Query's task cache.

**Risk**: Source-of-truth split between Supabase (canonical) and Zustand (derived cache). If task sync fails, the store goes stale with no recovery signal.

---

### 4. **selectionStore**
- **File**: `/shared/state/selectionStore.ts`
- **Store name**: `selectionStore`
- **State held**:
  - Gallery selection (map of IDs → metadata, clips, summary)
  - Timeline selection (selected/primary clip IDs, selected track, additive flag)
  - Shot selection (current shot, last affected, shot-addition selection)

- **How consumed**: Specialized hooks (`useGallerySelection()`, `useTimelineSelectionStore()`, `useCurrentShot()`) with `shallow` equality; wrapper `useTimelineMultiSelect()` exposes refs for mutation tracking; vanilla API via `useSelectionStoreApi()`
- **Scope**: `feature-scoped` (video-editor tool)
- **Verdict**: **appropriate** — ephemeral UI selection state (no server source-of-truth). Heavy use of `shallow` equality and ref-based sync for perf. The multi-select hook exposes layout refs and memoization — fine for tool-scoped state. **Concern**: Cross-slice clear-on-select logic (`clearTimelineSelection` clears gallery) suggests tight coupling; monitor for feature creep.

**Good pattern**: Gallery + Timeline slices can clear each other but are logically separated, suggesting the store is nearing complexity ceiling.

---

### 5. **createEntityStore** (factory)
- **File**: `/shared/state/createEntityStore.ts`
- **Store name**: Creates typed entity stores (e.g., color presets, settings)
- **State held**:
  - Per-entity records: settings, status (idle/loading/ready/saving/error), error, persisted flag
  - Dirty state tracking, pending persistence timers, text field deferred keys
  - Clean snapshot + saved settings for revert

- **How consumed**: Factory returns `useEntity(entityId)` hook and `getState()` API; hooks return `EntityView<T>` with actions (updateField, save, revert, reset); `bootstrap` for hydration from DB
- **Scope**: `infrastructure` (generic entity lifecycle — settings, prefs, form state)
- **Verdict**: **appropriate** — this is a well-designed abstraction for client-side entity editing with deferred persistence. Handles dirty state, revert, debounced saves, and text-field buffering. No server duplication — the store is the working copy, DB is source. **Good signs**: explicit `hasPersistedData` flag, `isDirty()`/`hasPendingPersistence()` queries, clear mutation/save semantics. Uses `shallow` in selector.

**Note**: Debounce + persistence scheduling is in-store, not middleware — fine for this level of concern.

---

### 6. **timelineStore**
- **File**: `/tools/video-editor/hooks/timelineStore.ts`
- **Store name**: `timelineStore` (created per-instance via `createTimelineStore()`)
- **State held**:
  - Data slice: resolvedConfig, device class, input modality, interaction policy, selected clips/track, composition size, scale, preferences (refs + state)
  - Ops slice: all timeline operations (select, drag, drop, resize, track management, generation asset registry)
  - Chrome slice: save status, render status, undo/redo, checkpoints
  - Playback slice: current time, preview ref, player container ref

- **How consumed**: Context provider (`TimelineStoreProvider`) wraps tree; hooks `useTimelineStoreApi()` (required), `useTimelineStoreApiSafe()` (optional); slice-specific hooks like `useTimelineDataSlice()`, `useTimelineOpsSlice()`; subscriber hooks with selectors + equality functions; safe variants return null if store not mounted
- **Scope**: `feature-scoped` (video-editor tool) but **cross-cutting within tool** (data, ops, chrome, playback are tightly coupled)
- **Verdict**: **questionable** — this is a **god store**. It holds UI state (interaction, refs), business logic (operations), render state, and playback state all in one tree. The context-provider pattern + sync methods suggest it's trying to bridge React Context and Zustand. 

**Issues**:
  - Ops slice holds 40+ functions (select, drag, delete, split, track ops, asset registry) — suggests it's a command bus, not state
  - Data slice mixes state (selectedClipId) with refs (dataRef, interactionStateRef) — some values should be local React state
  - Safe variants return null on unmount — breaks the required-provider pattern (see CLAUDE.md)
  - Bootstrap is manual (sync* methods called from parent), not automatic

**Consider**: Split into smaller stores (data state, playback, render status) + use React Context for ops dispatch. The current design loads 30+ no-op functions at rest; refactor toward lazy binding.

---

## Summary Table

| Store Name | Scope | Verdict | Key Issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| panesStore | global-ui | appropriate | None — well-designed lifecycle ownership |
| overlayStack | global-ui | appropriate | None — clean query API |
| realtimeStore | global-domain | questionable | Duplicates server state; consider React Query cache |
| selectionStore | feature-scoped | appropriate | Monitor for creep; slices are loosely coupled |
| createEntityStore | infrastructure | appropriate | Generic entity edit lifecycle works well |
| timelineStore | feature-scoped | questionable | God store; ops slice is 40+ functions; safe variants break required-provider pattern |

---

## Cross-Cutting Observations

1. **Equality discipline**: All stores using `shallow` or custom `equalityFn` in selectors — good practice prevents thrashing.
2. **No `subscribe` side effects**: None of the stores couple to imperative lifecycle (e.g., `.subscribe((state) => doSideEffect())`).
3. **No `useStoreWithEqualityFn` wrappers**: Stores expose purpose-built hooks (e.g., `useGallerySelection()`) rather than forcing consumers to write selectors — reduces boilerplate.
4. **Bootstrap pattern**: panesStore and timelineStore use manual bootstrap (via Provider or sync methods); createEntityStore uses explicit `bootstrapEntity()`. This is consistent but not automatic — consider a reusable Provider HOC.
5. **Migration targets (M1a/M1b/M1c)**: realtimeStore and timelineStore are the best candidates for review. realtimeStore should either commit to being a React Query cache layer or move fully to Query. timelineStore needs decomposition.

---

## Recommendations

- **realtimeStore**: If the "realtime" aspect is truly generation→task dependency tracking, that's valid. If it's just task caching, merge with React Query's `tasks` query.
- **timelineStore**: Extract Ops as a separate dispatch context. Move Refs to local state where possible. Break Data/Playback/Chrome into smaller stores if they have independent lifecycles.
- **selectionStore**: Watch slice coupling; if gallery ↔ timeline clears grow, consider event-driven reset or a unified "workspace clear" action.
- **panesStore + overlayStack**: Keep as-is; both are exemplary.
```

Is the value updated frequently (not just once at mount)? Is it consumed by many components (grep for the hook name)? If yes to all three, it's a zustand candidate.\n\nCall out specifically:\n- Contexts where `value={{ state, setState }}` pattern appears (classic context-as-store anti-pattern — re-renders all consumers on any change)\n- Contexts with >5 consumers that hold mutable state\n- \"Provider\" components more than 100 lines long (usually means embedded state logic worth extracting)\n\n**Pattern 2: Shared state living in useState hoisted to a high component + prop-drilled.**\nGrep for components that:\n- Have many `useState` calls (>5)\n- Pass state + setter as props to multiple child levels\n- Have names matching `*Layout.tsx`, `*Shell.tsx`, `*Orchestrator.tsx`, `*Manager.tsx`, `App.tsx`\n\nAlso find any module-level `let` variables that act as singletons for app state (e.g. `let currentThing: X | null = null` — usually a lifecycle bug waiting to happen, sometimes a zustand candidate).\n\nFor each candidate, report:\n- File path + line number + (context name or component name)\n- What the state holds (one line)\n- Why zustand would be better: \"N consumers re-render on any change\" / \"prop-drilled through M levels\" / \"module singleton, no React lifecycle\"\n- Risk/size of migration: `trivial` (<50 lines changed), `moderate` (50-200), `large` (>200 or crosses tool boundaries)\n\nBackground: the repo uses zustand for overlayStack, panesStore, and is migrating more under M1a/M1b/M1c commits. ## Top 10 Zustand Migration Candidates

**1. VideoEditorShell (FullEditorLayout)** — `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/VideoEditorShell.tsx` (lines 92–795, nested in 808-line file)
- **State**: 7 useState hooks managing layout state (timelineHeight, isTimelineMaximized, condensedRightPanel, isMobilePropertiesOpen, previewHostEl, tooSmall, previewActionButtonClass)
- **Why zustand**: State is deeply local to one component but frequently updated on user interaction (resizing, mode changes, preview toggling). No prop drilling, but encapsulates significant logic worth extracting for reuse across video-editor variants.
- **Risk**: `moderate` (150–200 lines changed; would split into layout + interaction substores)

**2. UserSettingsContext (mutable server state)** — `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/contexts/UserSettingsContext.tsx` (lines 43–154)
- **State**: userSettings object + isLoadingSettings, mutable via updateUserSettings callback
- **Consumers**: 4 direct uses found; context-as-store anti-pattern (updateUserSettings + setters)
- **Why zustand**: Server-persisted settings that sync cross-device. Zustand would separate read (query) from write (action) more cleanly; currently conflates fetch/update in one context.
- **Risk**: `moderate` (requires separating server sync from UI binding; ~100 lines)

**3. IncomingTasksContext (mutable placeholder queue)** — `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/contexts/IncomingTasksContext.tsx` (lines 65–159)
- **State**: incomingTasks array + cancellation tracking (useRef), 8 mutable actions (add/remove/resolve/cancel)
- **Consumers**: 7 direct uses (multiple generation creation flows)
- **Why zustand**: Classic state-as-queue anti-pattern. Many writers (task creation, cancellation) and readers (gallery, panes). Zustand would give atomic updates + computed cancellation flags.
- **Risk**: `trivial` (<50 lines; state shape fits zustand perfectly)

**4. ProjectContext (heavy tree of selection + derived state)** — `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/contexts/ProjectContext.tsx` (140 lines)
- **State**: selectedProjectId, projects list, computed derived selectors
- **Consumers**: 64 direct uses; re-renders all on selection change
- **Why zustand**: Very high consumer count. Selection is a core app singleton; zustand would decouple ID selector from list queries.
- **Risk**: `moderate` (64 consumers means broad change; but clean migration path)

**5. VideoTravelSettingsProvider (4-context tower)** — `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/providers/VideoTravelSettingsProvider.tsx` (150+ lines shown)
- **State**: settings object (150+ fields), status, isDirty, loading, handlers, save ops; split into 4 nested contexts
- **Why zustand**: Multi-domain settings with complex derived state (phase configs, LoRA selection, form validation). Four context layers screams "extract to store".
- **Risk**: `moderate` (state is well-shaped for zustand; would collapse 4 contexts to 1 store + selectors)

**6. LightboxStateContext (4-context value tree, no mutations)** — `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/contexts/LightboxStateContext.tsx` (lines 119–134)
- **State**: core, media, variants, navigation domains; handlers are no-ops when provider missing (pattern violation per CLAUDE.md)
- **Why zustand**: Safe hooks with fallback no-ops defeat provider-safety contracts. Zustand selectors would clarify intent (optional vs required).
- **Risk**: `moderate` (clean refactor; adds type safety)

**7. webRendererCache singleton** — `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClientRender.ts` (line 44)
- **State**: `let webRendererCache: WebRendererModule | null = null` — module-level singleton holding dynamically loaded renderer
- **Why zustand**: Lifetime leaks across renders. No React lifecycle management. Should use zustand + cleanup on unmount, or move to useMemo.
- **Risk**: `trivial` (<30 lines; simple migration)

**8. ImageEditFormContext (form state with setters)** — `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/contexts/ImageEditFormContext.tsx` (lines 54–117)
- **State**: 10+ form fields (prompts, strengths, LoRA, model) with individual setters
- **Pattern**: `{ state, setState }` repeated 10× (antipattern per your brief)
- **Why zustand**: Form state belongs in a store. Zustand action `updateForm({ inpaintPrompt, img2imgStrength, ... })` beats 10 separate setters.
- **Risk**: `trivial` (form shape is simple; few consumers)

**9. AIInputModeContext (mode + handlers)** — `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/contexts/AIInputModeContext.tsx` (42 lines)
- **State**: inputMode string + handler, updates on user mode switch
- **Consumers**: 14 direct uses
- **Why zustand**: Simple enum + handler. High consumer count for low state complexity. Zustand atoms would be more efficient.
- **Risk**: `trivial` (rename hook, move to zustand store)

**10. GenerationTaskContext (stateful caching)** — `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/contexts/GenerationTaskContext.tsx` (lines 38–80)
- **State**: isPreloadingEnabled boolean + mutable callbacks
- **Consumers**: Implicit (generation gallery surfaces consume cached task mappings)
- **Why zustand**: Preload configuration should be a persistent store setting, not re-created on every render. Zustand would let components memoize it.
- **Risk**: `trivial` (<30 lines)

---

**High-value candidates** (most migration impact):
1. **ProjectContext** (64 consumers, app-level selection)
2. **IncomingTasksContext** (7 consumers, queue anti-pattern)
3. **VideoTravelSettingsProvider** (nested towers, multi-domain)

**Quick wins** (easiest migrations):
- webRendererCache singleton
- AIInputModeContext
- ImageEditFormContext

Duplicating server state.** If a store holds arrays/objects fetched from Supabase (anything with fields like `id`, `created_at`, `*_id`, etc. that look like rows), it's probably fighting React Query. Flag it. Hint: grep for usages of `supabase` or `queryClient` inside the same file as a zustand store creator.\n\n**B. Missing selectors / over-subscription.** Stores consumed via `useStore((s) => s)` (returning the whole state) or `useStore()` with no selector force re-renders on any field change. Grep for both patterns near zustand hook calls.\n\n**C. Missing `shallow` / equality function on composite selectors.** If a selector returns an object/array (e.g. `(s) => ({ a: s.a, b: s.b })`) without `shallow` or a custom equality fn, every render produces a new reference and the consumer re-renders every store change. Look for selector functions returning `{` or `[` literals and check whether the hook call passes `shallow` (from `zustand/shallow`) or an equality function.\n\n**D. Global state that should be per-instance.** Any store that holds state that depends on route params, IDs, or feature context (e.g. \"current project ID\", \"current shot ID\") at the module level. Look for stores whose fields include `currentXId` / `activeXId` / `selectedXId` where X is a domain entity.\n\n**E. `subscribe` with side effects.** Stores that call `.subscribe()` at module scope or inside a Bootstrap provider to trigger side effects (toasts, fetches, DOM manipulation). These couple the store to the app lifecycle in ways that break under HMR and SSR. Flag them.\n\n**F. Zombie state / missing lifecycle.** State that should reset on navigation/logout/user-switch but lives module-level in zustand with no reset hook. Hint: grep for stores that have no `reset` or `clear` method but hold user-specific data.\n\n**G. Perfect! Now let me check line 45-46 of selectionStore more carefully and search for the `activeTaskId` field and its usage:

Based on my thorough analysis of the zustand stores in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src`, here are the anti-patterns I found:

## Zustand Anti-Pattern Findings

### D. Global state that should be per-instance

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/state/selectionStore.ts`
**Line:** 45-46, 377-382
**Pattern:** Pattern D – Global state holding instance IDs
```typescript
activeTaskId: string | null;
setActiveTaskId: (taskId: string | null) => void;
```
**Evidence:** The store holds `activeTaskId` (line 45), `currentShotId` (line 59), and `lastAffectedShotId` (line 60) at module scope. These are entity IDs that depend on route context. If two task modals or shot editors can open in parallel (e.g., sidebar + main view), this state forces them to conflict since only one `activeTaskId` exists globally.
**Severity:** `bug` – Visible incorrect behavior when multiple instances of the same entity type are active.
**Suggested fix:** Move `activeTaskId` to route params or a per-modal Context; keep only selection-related state (clips, gallery items) in the global store.

---

### D. Global state that should be per-instance

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/state/panesStore.ts`
**Line:** 45-46, 377-382
**Pattern:** Pattern D – Global state holding instance IDs
```typescript
activeTaskId: string | null;
setActiveTaskId: (taskId: string | null) => void;
```
**Evidence:** `panesStore` also duplicates `activeTaskId` (line 45) and setter (line 46), making it a second source of truth for the same entity ID. Updates to one store don't sync with the other, causing desync between pane layout and task selection state.
**Severity:** `bug` – State divergence between two stores tracking the same ID.
**Suggested fix:** Consolidate `activeTaskId` into a single store (or URL/Context); remove it from `panesStore`.

---

### A. Duplicating server state

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/state/realtimeStore.ts`
**Line:** 15–20, 268–327
**Pattern:** Pattern A – Store duplicates Task rows fetched from Supabase
```typescript
interface RealtimeTaskScopeState {
  tasks: Record<string, Task>;  // Task[] from Supabase has id, created_at, *_id fields
  ...
}
const realtimeTaskStore = createStore<RealtimeStoreState>((set, get) => ({
  scopes: {},
  upsertTask: (task, projectId) => { ... },
  upsertTasks: (tasks, projectId) => { ... },
  ...
}));
```
**Evidence:** Lines 268–327 define a vanilla store holding `tasks: Record<string, Task>`, where `Task` is a Supabase row with fields like `id`, `created_at`, `status`, etc. This is cache-level state duplication. React Query should own `Task` entities; zustand should hold only derived/local state (e.g., "pending pending tasks for a generation"). The store calls `mapTaskDbRowToTask()` (line 390) to convert rows, indicating direct database-to-store flow with no React Query involvement.
**Severity:** `perf` + `smell` – Unnecessary duplication creates a stale-data risk; React Query is the correct owner.
**Suggested fix:** Remove Task entities from zustand; use React Query for server state; keep only generation-to-pending-task mappings in zustand.

---

### C. Missing `shallow` / equality function on composite selectors

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/state/panesStore.ts`
**Line:** 440
**Pattern:** Pattern C – Selector returns object literal without shallow
```typescript
export function usePanesStoreAvailability(): PanesStoreAvailability {
  return usePanesStore((state) => state.availability, shallow);
}
```
**Evidence:** This hook correctly uses `shallow` (line 440), but is listed for context. However, examining line 467–510, the `useBootstrapPanesStore()` hook builds a large bootstrap input object (`bootstrapInput`) with many function references. When these callbacks change, the object is recreated. The effect at line 517–520 then re-runs, calling `bootstrapPanesStore()` again even though the underlying pane state hasn't changed. This is a reference-identity leak, not directly a selector problem, but a related anti-pattern.
**Severity:** `perf` – Unnecessary re-bootstraps on every prop change.
**Suggested fix:** Memoize callbacks or use a stable key to deduplicate bootstrap updates (already partially mitigated by `areBootstrapInputsEqual` check at line 391, but callback references still cause thrashing).

---

### G. Redundant stores

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/state/selectionStore.ts` and `panesStore.ts`
**Line:** selectionStore line 45, panesStore line 45
**Pattern:** Pattern G – Two stores holding the same field
**Evidence:** Both `selectionStore` and `panesStore` define `activeTaskId`. This violates single-source-of-truth; updates to one are invisible to the other. This is a design smell that forces callers to check both stores or manually sync them.
**Severity:** `smell` – Maintenance burden; easy to forget to update both.
**Suggested fix:** Remove `activeTaskId` from one store; use a single source of truth (ideally URL or Context).

---

### Summary (by severity)

| Issue | File | Line | Pattern | Severity |
|-------|------|------|---------|----------|
| `activeTaskId` global state conflicts | selectionStore.ts | 45–46, 377 | D | **bug** |
| `activeTaskId` duplicate in panesStore | panesStore.ts | 45–46, 377 | D + G | **bug** |
| Task rows duplicated from Supabase | realtimeStore.ts | 15–20, 268 | A | **perf** |
| Bootstrap object ref churn | panesStore.ts | 482–510 | C (related) | **perf** |

**Patterns NOT found:**
- **B (over-subscription):** All hook calls use explicit selectors (e.g., `useTimelineDataSelector((state) => ({}), shallow)`); no bare `useStore()` calls detected.
- **E (module-scope `.subscribe()` side effects):** All store subscriptions are inside React Effects or hooks; no module-level side effects detected.
- **F (missing reset/lifecycle):** All stores have `reset`/`clear`/`resetForProjectChange` methods (e.g., `__resetSelectionStoreForTests`, `__resetPanesStoreForTests`).

Three-way audit done. Here's the synthesis, with one finding I had to flag as hallucinated:

## What's already in zustand (6 stores)

| Store | Verdict | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `panesStore` | ✅ appropriate | Exemplary — own your future stores after this one |
| `overlayStack` | ✅ appropriate | Clean, post-cleanup |
| `selectionStore` | ✅ appropriate | Slice coupling worth watching, otherwise fine |
| `createEntityStore` (factory) | ✅ appropriate | Good abstraction for entity-edit lifecycle |
| `realtimeStore` | ⚠️ questionable | Holds `Record<string, Task>` of Supabase rows — **duplicates server state** alongside React Query. Perf risk + staleness. Either commit to being a computed layer over Query cache, or move to Query entirely. **Verified real.** |
| `timelineStore` | ⚠️ questionable | God store: 4 slices (data, ops, chrome, playback) + 40+ action functions + refs mixed with state. Safe variants return null, which per your own CLAUDE.md is a pattern violation. Decomposition candidate. |

Cross-cutting good signs: all stores use `shallow` or custom equality, all have reset/lifecycle hooks, no module-level `.subscribe` side effects, no bare `useStore()` without selectors.

## Top migration candidates (not in zustand yet)

**High value:**
1. **`ProjectContext`** — 64 direct consumers, selection state in React Context. Every `setSelectedProjectId` re-renders everything. Zustand + selectors would shed most of that.
2. **`IncomingTasksContext`** — queue-in-context anti-pattern (7 consumers). Classic zustand shape.
3. **`VideoTravelSettingsProvider`** — four nested contexts for one feature's settings. Collapse to a single store + slice selectors.

**Quick wins:**
- `webRendererCache` — module-level `let` singleton in `useClientRender.ts`, no lifecycle.
- `AIInputModeContext` — trivial enum + handler, 14 consumers.
- `ImageEditFormContext` — `{ state, setState }` × 10 fields. Form → store is a cleaner idiom.

**Skip / leave alone:** anything in `media-lightbox` contexts (mostly DI, not mutable state), and the existing pane/overlay stores.

## Flag I had to shoot down

One subagent reported `activeTaskId` duplicated across both `panesStore` *and* `selectionStore`, citing identical line numbers in each file. That duplication doesn't exist — `activeTaskId` is only in `panesStore` (line 45); `selectionStore` has no task fields at all. Hallucinated. Mentioning because the "sync bug" sounded concrete enough to act on.

## Suggested ordering

Given you're in the middle of `m1b/m1c` on selection + timeline: finish that milestone, then before M1d (or whatever's next), spend a day on `timelineStore` decomposition — that store is on a trajectory that will make future slices painful. `realtimeStore` is a bigger decision (React Query vs. zustand ownership) and probably wants its own mini-plan rather than being bolted into an existing milestone.

Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile the report:

## **Z-Index and Locked Panels Analysis Report**

### **1. What is a "Locked Panel"?**

A locked panel is a sidebar/pane (TasksPane, GenerationsPane, etc.) that remains persistently visible on screen. The `panesStore` tracks lock state via flags like `isTasksPaneLocked`, `isGenerationsPaneLocked`, etc. When locked, panes stay open and render above other content.

**Locked pane components:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/features/tasks/components/TasksPane/TasksPane.tsx` (lines 162–172): Container div with `zIndex: 100001` when locked
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/features/gallery/components/GenerationsPane/GenerationsPane.tsx` (line 105): Uses `zIndex: UI_Z_LAYERS.GENERATIONS_PANE` (100)

### **2. LightboxShell Z-Index Hierarchy**

From `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/components/LightboxShell.tsx`:
- **Backdrop**: `LightboxDialogBackdrop` → `getOverlayLayerStyle(layer, 'backdrop', ...)` → z-index = **1000 + (layer × 10) + 0**
- **Popup (content)**: `LightboxDialogPopup` → `getOverlayLayerStyle(layer, 'popup', ...)` → z-index = **1000 + (layer × 10) + 2**

The comment (line 11) mentions "PaneControlTab... visible above the lightbox at z-[100001]".

### **3. Pane Z-Indices and Rendering**

| Pane | Z-Index | Locked | Rendering | File |
|------|---------|--------|-----------|------|
| **TasksPane** | 100001 | Yes | Fixed div portal, non-modal | TasksPane.tsx:172 |
| **TasksPane Backdrop** | 100000 | Mobile | Portal, dismisses pane | TasksPane.tsx:144 |
| **GenerationsPane** | 100 | Yes | Fixed bottom surface | GenerationsPane.tsx:105 |
| **GenerationsPane Backdrop** | 99 | Mobile | Fixed inset-0 | GenerationsPane.tsx:25 |
| **GenerationsPane Control Tab** | 101–102 (CONTROL_LOCKED/UNLOCKED) | When lock/unlock visible | Fixed, portaled | PaneControlTab.tsx:314–315 |
| **TasksPane Control Tab** | 100011 (desktop) / 99 (mobile) | Always visible when pane visible | Fixed, portaled | PaneControlTab.tsx:311–313 (uses UI_Z_LAYERS) |
| **Lightbox Backdrop** | ~100010 (layer 1) | N/A | Modal overlay | getOverlayLayerStyle formula: 1000 + 10 + 0 |
| **Lightbox Popup** | ~100012 (layer 1) | N/A | Modal overlay | getOverlayLayerStyle formula: 1000 + 10 + 2 |

**Current formula** (shared.tsx:38–40):
```
zIndex = OVERLAY_BASE_Z_INDEX (1000) + layer × OVERLAY_LAYER_STEP (10) + OVERLAY_LAYER_SLOT_OFFSET[slot]
```
With layer=1 (typical), this gives backdrop=1010, popup=1012. But `UI_Z_LAYERS.LIGHTBOX_MODAL = 100010` suggests a different calculation path.

### **4. Recent Changes (Commit f06a5d787)**

The critical regression was introduced in commit `f06a5d787` "chore: overlay pointer-events cleanup". Key changes:

**Deletions that affected z-index/pointer visibility:**

1. **LightboxLayout.tsx (line 406)**: Deleted `pointer-events-auto` class
   ```diff
   - core.isMobile && imageEdit.isInpaintMode && 'pointer-events-auto',
   ```
   This deleted conditional `pointer-events-auto` on the mobile inpaint canvas background, which allowed the lightbox to receive pointer events when the canvas was active.

2. **WorkflowControlsBar.tsx (line 43)**: Deleted `pointer-events-auto` from controls bar
   ```diff
   - <div className="absolute bottom-24 left-1/2 transform -translate-x-1/2 flex items-center z-[70] pointer-events-auto">
   + <div className="absolute bottom-24 left-1/2 transform -translate-x-1/2 flex items-center z-[70]">
   ```

3. **LightboxShell.tsx (line 79)**: Deleted `handleOverlayClick` handler and removed `onClick={handleOverlayClick}` from backdrop.

**The bug:** When a locked panel is open (e.g., TasksPane at z-100001), and the lightbox opens (z-100010 modal), the lightbox *should* appear above the pane. However, the deletion of `pointer-events-auto` from the lightbox background in inpaint mode combined with the removal of the click handler means:
- The lightbox backdrop loses explicit pointer-events declaration
- Without `pointer-events-auto`, it may inherit `pointer-events-none` from its parent context
- Locked panes (z-100001) now visually/interactively block the lightbox (z-100010) on certain viewport sizes

### **5. getOverlayLayerStyle Formula**

From `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/ui/overlay/shared.tsx`:
```typescript
const OVERLAY_BASE_Z_INDEX = 1000;
const OVERLAY_LAYER_STEP = 10;
const OVERLAY_LAYER_SLOT_OFFSET = { backdrop: 0, positioner: 1, popup: 2 };

// Formula: zIndex = 1000 + layer*10 + slot_offset
```

For typical lightbox (layer=1): **backdrop=1010, popup=1012**
But TasksPane locked container = **100001** (orders of magnitude higher).

---

### **Root-Cause Hypothesis**

Commit f06a5d787 deleted the `pointer-events-auto` class from the lightbox background layer (LightboxLayout.tsx:406) during the pointer-events cleanup. Combined with the removal of the backdrop click handler, this left the lightbox interactive surface without explicit pointer-events, causing it to render *behind* the locked TasksPane (100001) when pointer-events inheritance propagates downward. The lightbox modal (z-100010) is still layered above the pane in CSS stacking order, but interaction events are blocked.

**One-line fix candidates:**
1. **LightboxLayout.tsx:406** — Restore the conditional `pointer-events-auto`:
   ```tsx
   core.isMobile && imageEdit.isInpaintMode && 'pointer-events-auto',
   ```
2. **LightboxShell.tsx:130** — Explicitly add `pointerEvents: 'auto'` to backdrop style to ensure it remains interactive
3. **shared.tsx:38** — Ensure `getOverlayLayerStyle` always sets `pointerEvents: 'auto'` for backdrop/popup slots when layer is set
```

+import * as React from 'react';
+
+const OVERLAY_BASE_Z_INDEX = 1000;
+const OVERLAY_LAYER_STEP = 10;
+
+const OVERLAY_LAYER_SLOT_OFFSET = {
+  backdrop: 0,
+  positioner: 1,
+  popup: 2,
+} as const;
+
+type OverlayLayerSlot = keyof typeof OVERLAY_LAYER_SLOT_OFFSET;
+
+export function composeRefs<T>(
+  ...refs: Array<React.Ref<T> | undefined | null>
+): React.RefCallback<T> {
+  return (node) => {
+    refs.forEach((ref) => {
+      if (typeof ref === 'function') {
+        ref(node);
+      } else if (ref) {
+        (ref as React.MutableRefObject<T | null>).current = node;
+      }
+    });
+  };
+}
+
+export function getOverlayLayerStyle(
+  layer: number | null,
+  slot: OverlayLayerSlot,
+  style?: React.CSSProperties,
+): React.CSSProperties {
+  const resolvedLayer = Math.max(layer ?? 1, 1);
+
+  return {
+    ...(style ?? {}),
+    zIndex:
+      OVERLAY_BASE_Z_INDEX +
+      resolvedLayer * OVERLAY_LAYER_STEP +
+      OVERLAY_LAYER_SLOT_OFFSET[slot],
+  };
+}

1	import { useMemo } from 'react';

7	interface UseLightboxPaneLayoutArgs {

9	}

11	interface LightboxPaneLayoutStyles {

13	  contentStyle: React.CSSProperties;
14	}
15	
16	export function useLightboxPaneLayout({
17	  overlayViewport,
18	}: UseLightboxPaneLayoutArgs): LightboxPaneLayoutStyles {
19	  return useMemo(() => {
20	    const accountForTasksPane = shouldAccountForTasksPane(overlayViewport);
21	
22	    const overlayStyle: React.CSSProperties = {
23	      pointerEvents: 'all',
24	      touchAction: 'none',
25	      cursor: 'pointer',
26	      zIndex: 10000,
27	      position: 'fixed',
28	      top: 0,
29	      left: 0,
30	      right: accountForTasksPane ? `${overlayViewport.tasksPaneWidth}px` : 0,
31	      bottom: 0,
32	      transition: 'right 300ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1), width 300ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1)',
33	      ...(accountForTasksPane
34	        ? { width: `calc(100vw - ${overlayViewport.tasksPaneWidth}px)` }

36	    };
37

39	      transition: 'width 300ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1)',

41	        ? { width: `calc(100vw - ${overlayViewport.tasksPaneWidth}px)` }
42	        : overlayViewport.needsFullscreenLayout
43	        ? { width: '100vw' }
44	        : {}),
45	    };
46	
47	    return { overlayStyle, contentStyle };
48	  }, [overlayViewport]);
49	}

const LightboxDialogBackdrop = React.forwardRef<
  HTMLDivElement,
  React.ComponentPropsWithoutRef<typeof DialogPrimitive.Backdrop>
>(({ style, ...props }, ref) => {
  const { layer, registerElement } = useLightboxDialogLayerContext();
  const backdropRef = React.useMemo(
    () => composeRefs(ref, (node: HTMLDivElement | null) => registerElement('backdrop', node)),
    [ref, registerElement],
  );

  return (
    <DialogPrimitive.Backdrop
      ref={backdropRef}
      style={getOverlayLayerStyle(layer, 'backdrop', style)}
      {...props}
    />
  );
});
LightboxDialogBackdrop.displayName = 'LightboxDialogBackdrop';

const LightboxDialogPopup = React.forwardRef<
  HTMLDivElement,
  React.ComponentPropsWithoutRef<typeof DialogPrimitive.Popup>
>(({ style, ...props }, ref) => {
  const { layer, registerElement } = useLightboxDialogLayerContext();
  const popupRef = React.useMemo(
    () => composeRefs(ref, (node: HTMLDivElement | null) => registerElement('popup', node)),
    [ref, registerElement],
  );

  return (
    <DialogPrimitive.Popup
      ref={popupRef}
      style={getOverlayLayerStyle(layer, 'popup', style)}
      {...props}
    />
  );
});
LightboxDialogPopup.displayName = 'LightboxDialogPopup';

> AGENT

Got it. Root cause pinpointed.

**The bug:** `GenerationsPane` (at `UI_Z_LAYERS.GENERATIONS_PANE = 100`) sits below the lightbox popup/backdrop (computed z = ~1010–1012 via `getOverlayLayerStyle`). TasksPane works fine (z = 100001), but `GenerationsPane` — which is what you're seeing get hidden — was explicitly pinned low in `uiLayers.ts` by the M2 snapshot's z-layer contract, and the test file (`uiLayers.test.ts`) even codifies "lightbox > generations_pane" as the invariant.

Fix: bump `GENERATIONS_PANE` (and its backdrop) above the lightbox, update the test.

> AGENT

Pushed `eb010e83d`.

## Root cause & fix

**What was happening:** `GenerationsPane` was pinned at z-index `100` (backdrop at `99`) in `uiLayers.ts`. The lightbox renders at computed z ~1012 via `getOverlayLayerStyle`. So locking the pane while a lightbox is open buried the pane under the lightbox.

**Why TasksPane was fine:** its locked body is hardcoded `zIndex: 100001` directly in `TasksPane.tsx`, not from `uiLayers.ts`. That's already above the lightbox.

**Fix:** bumped `GENERATIONS_PANE_BACKDROP: 100012`, `GENERATIONS_PANE: 100013`, and `TASKS_PANE_TAB_ABOVE_LIGHTBOX: 100014` (so the tab still sits above the Generations pane). Test updated — the old invariant asserted `LIGHTBOX_MODAL > GENERATIONS_PANE`, which was exactly the bug codified.

**Not fixed in this commit:** `HOME_GLASS_PANE` is still at `100`. If lightboxes can open on the Home page over `CreativePartnerPane`, that'd show the same symptom. Flag it if you hit it.

> AGENT

Let me ask before making more changes — "stuff" is ambiguous. A few interpretations:

1. **The lightbox content itself is clipped/hidden behind the pane's edge** (spatial overlap) — fix is updating `useLightboxPaneLayout` to offset for Generations/Shots panes, not just Tasks.
2. **Pane control tabs** (the little tab you click to open/close the pane) are still at z=101/102 — still below the lightbox (~1012), so they're hidden when a lightbox is open.
3. **Other panes (ToolsPane at z=60, ShotsPane)** weren't touched by my fix and still render behind lightboxes.
4. **Modals (Settings, Dialogs) now go behind the panes** because I raised panes above the dialog z-range.

Which one are you seeing? If you can describe it as "when I do X, Y shows up behind Z" that'd nail it.

Or if you can share a screenshot / rough description of which element is winning the stack when it shouldn't, I can go straight to the fix.

> AGENT

Pushed `9c84dabde`.

## Final hierarchy

```
120000  Toast                            (top)
110010  Dialog / AlertDialog / Popover / Select / etc.  (layer 1)
100014  Pane tab above lightbox
100013  GenerationsPane body
100012  GenerationsPane backdrop
100001  TasksPane body (hardcoded)
100000  TasksPane backdrop (hardcoded)
  1010  Lightbox backdrop
  1012  Lightbox popup                   (bottom-ish)
```

Key changes:
- `OVERLAY_BASE_Z_INDEX: 1000 → 110000` so every modal/popover/select/dropdown now lands above locked panes
- Added exported `LIGHTBOX_BASE_Z_INDEX: 1000`; `lightbox.tsx` passes it explicitly so the lightbox stays below panes (matches the earlier "panes in front of lightbox" ask)
- `TOAST_VIEWPORT: 100020 → 120000` so toasts still clear modals

Reload and confirm. The `string | boolean` TS error in `lightbox.tsx:28` is pre-existing and unrelated — leaving it for a separate cleanup.

> AGENT

Pushed `4977aa022`.

## Cause

When `showBackdrop` fires (mobile, pane open, not locked), the backdrop at `z=100012` covered the pane control tab at `z=101/102`. The tab is rendered via the shared `PaneControlTab` component using class-based z-indices from `PANE_CONFIG.zIndex.CONTROL_LOCKED/UNLOCKED` — both were set way too low once I bumped the GenerationsPane body/backdrop into the 100012-100013 range.

## Fix

`CONTROL_LOCKED: 'z-[101]' → 'z-[100015]'`
`CONTROL_UNLOCKED: 'z-[102]' → 'z-[100016]'`

Both now clear the GenerationsPane surface. TasksPane tab already uses its own inline `zIndex: TASKS_PANE_TAB_ABOVE_LIGHTBOX` (100014) — unaffected. Modals at 110010+ still cover tabs, which matches your "modals in front of everything" ask.

> AGENT

Pushed `bc9aca1a7`.

## Cause

`deriveLayout` called `getEffectivePaneHeights` with `idealEditor = 50% viewport`, `generations = stored-height (default 350)`. When those fit (e.g., 540+350 = 890 ≤ 1080), each pane kept its preferred size, leaving ~190px uncovered in the middle.

## Fix

When both `isEditorPaneLocked && isGenerationsPaneLocked`, bypass the "preferred heights" logic and hard-split:

- Editor: `round(viewport * 0.5)`
- Generations: `viewport - editorHeight`

Open-but-not-locked behavior is unchanged — preferred heights still respected there.
